MC	<p>98a3_c006</p> <h3>Genotype Diversity in Hybrid Cross</h3><p>Firdose and Jasmine are crossed. Use their genotypes to determine the number of different genotypes possible in their offspring.</p><p>Assume that all genes sort independently and display complete dominance.</p><p><i>Hint: For each gene pair from the parents, you can get 1, 2, or 3 unique genotypes.<br/>This is based on the combinations: homozygous x homozygous (1), homozygous x heterozygous (2), and heterozygous x heterozygous (3).</i></p><p>Considering the principle of independent assortment, how many unique <span style="color: MediumBlue;"><strong>GENOTYPES</strong></span> could be produced in a hybrid cross between the following individuals?</p><p>Use the blank table to organize your work. For each gene, enter the number of possibilities, then multiply across the row to find the total.</p><p>Work in order: (a) and (b) count the different gametes each person can produce; (c) give the Punnett square dimensions (rows &times; columns) and total number of cells; then count the different offspring outcomes in the remaining row(s). You do not need to draw the full Punnett square.</p><p>Fill in the white cells and shaded TOTAL cells.
